<img width="1608" height="1764" alt="Image" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/af7d141b-295e-4d54-ba78-d9455a1f61e1" />15:T542,## Description

Integrate N8N workflow engine as the primary automation layer for Eliza App. N8N provides pre-built nodes for Gmail, Notion, Calendar, and hundreds of other services.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] N8N plugin from V2 branch working in cloud
- [ ] Agent can generate workflow JSONs via natural language
- [ ] Workflows can be stored per user/tenant
- [ ] Execution via [N8N.io](<http://N8N.io>) or self-hosted execution server
- [ ] Query available nodes and expose to agent

## Technical Notes

**From today's meeting (Shaw):**

* N8N plugin already exists in V2 branch - generates JSON workflows
* "We get every single thing" - Gmail, Notion, Reddit, literally every connector exists
* Two parts: (1) Generate JSON workflows (no sandbox needed), (2) Execute via N8N server
* Can use [N8N.io](<http://N8N.io>) hosted execution initially, self-host later for cost optimization
* Workflow generation is just JSON - can happen serverlessly
* Also need to pull in pre-existing workflows from GitHub (many already exist)
* "I really think we could get this done today" - Shaw

## Architecture

1. User says "check my gmail every morning"
2. Agent generates N8N workflow JSON
3. Agent requests OAuth credentials if needed
4. Workflow submitted to N8N execution server
5. Results returned to user

**Repository:** https://github.com/tdnupe3/coinrailz-eliza-plugin  
**Type:** x402 protocol infrastructure plugin  
**Status:** Production-ready

### Overview
This plugin adds x402 micropayment protocol support for ElizaOS agents, enabling autonomous payment for API services using USDC on Base mainnet.

**Technical Capabilities:**
- Multi-chain balance queries
- Gas price monitoring across chains
- Token price feeds and metadata
- Smart contract analysis
- Wallet risk scoring
- DEX liquidity data
- Transaction building utilities
- Cross-chain bridging

**Protocol:** x402 (HTTP 402 Payment Required) with Coinbase CDP facilitator  
**Network:** Base mainnet  
**Payment Token:** USDC

### Technical Features
✅ Full TypeScript implementation with type definitions  
✅ Automatic x402 payment handling  
✅ Coinbase CDP facilitator integration  
✅ Comprehensive documentation

### Installation
```bash
npm install github:tdnupe3/coinrailz-eliza-plugin

Usage

import { coinrailzPlugin } from 'coinrailz-eliza-plugin';

const agent = new Agent({
  plugins: [coinrailzPlugin]
});

Please review for inclusion in the ElizaOS plugin ecosystem.17:T783,# Untracked repositories table has 2x more columns than tracked repositories

## Current State

**Tracked repos (8 columns):**
- Core: `repoId`, `owner`, `name`, `description`
- Metrics: `stars`, `forks`
- Timestamps: `lastFetchedAt`, `lastUpdated`

**Untracked repos (18 columns):**
- Core: `repoId`, `owner`, `name`, `description`
- Metrics: `stars`, `forks`, `watchers`
- Metadata: `isArchived`, `primaryLanguage`
- Timestamps: `lastUpdatedAt`, `lastPushedAt`, `lastFetchedAt`
- Activity: `openPrCount`, `mergedPrCount`, `closedUnmergedPrCount`, `openIssueCount`, `closedIssueCount`
- Score: `activityScore`

## Issue

The "lightweight" untracked repos table has **18 columns** vs **8 columns** in tracked repos (2.25x more).

## Why It Happened

**Tracked repos**: Normalized architecture
- Minimal `repositories` table
- Separate `rawPullRequests`, `rawIssues`, `rawCommits` tables with full details

**Untracked repos**: Denormalized architecture  
- Everything in one table (no separate PR/issue tables)
- Stores counts and scores directly

## Potential Optimization

Could reduce to **9 essential columns**:
```sql
repoId, owner, name, description,
stars, forks,
lastUpdatedAt,  -- for delta detection
activityScore,  -- pre-computed
lastFetchedAt
```

**Trade-offs:**
- ✅ Simpler schema (9 vs 18 columns)
- ✅ Matches tracked repos complexity
- ❌ Lose tri-split PR/issue counts in DB (would need to recompute for UI)
- ❌ Lose metadata (language, archived status)

## Questions

1. Do we need tri-split counts stored, or can we compute on-the-fly?
2. Is `primaryLanguage` valuable for filtering/display?
3. Is `isArchived` needed or already filtered during ingestion?

## Non-Goal

This is not urgent - the denormalized approach works fine. Just documenting for future consideration.

## References

- Schema: `src/lib/data/schema.ts` lines 859-896
- Discovery: `src/lib/pipelines/discoverUntrackedRepos.ts`
18:T1435,## Problem Statement

We currently have 43 tracked repositories and 286 untracked repositories across the `elizaos` and `elizaos-plugins` organizations. The `config/example.json` file that determines which repos get full tracking (PRs, issues, commits, contributors, scoring) is manually maintained.

**Challenges:**
- Some tracked repos haven't been updated in months (low activity)
- Active untracked repos could benefit from full contributor analytics
- Manual config updates are time-consuming and error-prone
- No systematic way to identify promotion/demotion candidates

## Research Summary

Comprehensive research explored 6 strategies for automating config management. Full details in plan document: `/home/jin/.claude/plans/ticklish-finding-kitten.md`

### Strategy Comparison

| Strategy | Automation | Risk | Complexity | Effort | API Impact |
|----------|-----------|------|------------|--------|------------|
| 1. Threshold Auto | High | Medium | Medium | 8-12h | Reduces tracked |
| 2. Manual Review Dashboard | Low | None | Low | 8-11h | None |
| 3. Tiered Tracking | High | Medium | High | 21-27h | Reduces overall |
| 4. Adaptive Refresh | Medium | Low | Low | 6-9h | Reduces refresh |
| **5. CI/CD PR** ⭐ | Medium | Low | Medium | **9-13h** | Reduces tracked |
| 6. Hybrid Auto/Manual | Medium | Low | Medium | 10-14h | Reduces tracked |

### Recommended Approach

**Short-term (Quick Win):** Strategy 2 - Manual Review Dashboard
- Generate weekly report of stale tracked repos and active untracked repos
- Dashboard UI for human review
- Low risk, immediate value
- **Effort: 8-11 hours**

**Long-term (Automation):** Strategy 5 - CI/CD Automated Config PRs
- Weekly GitHub Actions workflow evaluates repos
- Auto-generates PRs with promotion/demotion recommendations
- Rich context for human review (activity trends, contributor counts, recent commits)
- Requires approval before merging
- **Effort: 9-13 hours**

### Key Technical Findings

**Reusable Code Identified:**
- ✅ `calculateActivityScore()` - Already exists in `src/lib/pipelines/untracked/mutations.ts`
- ✅ Activity score formula: log-scale stars/watchers, weighted PRs/issues, recency bonus
- ✅ Database batch operations pattern
- ✅ Time interval filtering utilities
- ✅ GitHub CLI PR creation patterns
- ✅ Config schema validation (Zod)

**Activity Score Formula:**
```typescript
activityScore = baseScore * archivePenalty

baseScore =
  log(stars + 1) * 1.0 +          // Log-scaled popularity
  log(watchers + 1) * 1.5 +       // Stronger engagement
  openPrCount * 3 +               // Active development (highest weight)
  mergedPrCount * 2 +             // Productive activity
  closedUnmergedPrCount * 1 +     // Engagement
  openIssueCount * 2 +            // Active discussions
  closedIssueCount * 1 +          // Maintenance
  recencyBonus                    // Max 14 points, decays over 14 days

archivePenalty = 0.5 if archived, else 1.0
```

### Implementation Outline (Strategy 5)

**Phase 1: Core Evaluation**
- Calculate activity scores for tracked repos (reuse existing formula)
- Build evaluation logic with hysteresis thresholds
- CLI command for testing: `bun run pipeline evaluate-tracking`

**Phase 2: PR Automation**
- Config update logic (read/modify `config/example.json`)
- Rich markdown PR description with activity details
- Preview comments showing recent contributor activity
- GitHub Actions workflow (weekly schedule + manual trigger)

**Phase 3: Refinement**
- Threshold tuning based on initial recommendations
- Testing with various scenarios
- Documentation and runbook for reviewing PRs

**Proposed Config:**
```json
{
  "PIPELINE_AUTO_CONFIG_PR": {
    "enabled": true,
    "schedule": "0 0 * * 0",
    "promoteThreshold": 45.0,
    "demoteThreshold": 12.0,
    "minimumTrackedDays": 60,
    "maxPromotionsPerPR": 5,
    "maxDemotionsPerPR": 3,
    "excludeFromDemotion": ["elizaos/eliza", "elizaos/docs"],
    "prLabels": ["automated", "config-management"],
    "requireApprovals": 1
  }
}
```

**Files to Create/Modify:**
- `src/lib/pipelines/tracking/calculateTrackedActivity.ts` - Activity score for tracked repos
- `src/lib/pipelines/tracking/evaluateRepos.ts` - Evaluation logic
- `cli/commands/evaluateTracking.ts` - CLI command
- `cli/commands/generateConfigPR.ts` - PR generation
- `.github/workflows/auto-config-pr.yml` - GitHub Actions workflow
- `src/lib/pipelines/pipelineConfig.ts` - Add AutoConfigPRSchema
- `config/example.json` - Add configuration

### Success Metrics

- Tracked repo count stabilizes or decreases
- High-activity untracked repos promoted within 1-2 weeks
- Low-activity tracked repos demoted within 1 month
- PR reviews take < 10 minutes
- No false positives (demoting active repos)

## Next Steps

1. Decide on implementation timeline
2. Set activity score thresholds
3. Identify exclude list (critical repos that should never be demoted)
4. Implement and test chosen strategy

- Untracked repos pipeline: #229 (merged)19:T495,## Description

Investigate and resolve the 3-second MCP plugin initialization delay on Vercel due to WebSocket handshake requirements.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Understand root cause of 3-second delay
- [ ] Research options: Redis cache, Gateway pattern, or dedicated server
- [ ] Implement solution that keeps connection alive
- [ ] Test performance improvement

## Technical Notes

**From today's meeting (Ben):**

* MCP plugin takes 3 seconds to initialize on every message
* Caused by needing to establish WebSocket communication with server
* Vercel serverless functions don't maintain state between invocations
* Options discussed:
  1. Redis cache for connection state (but WebSocket still needs reconnect)
  2. Gateway pattern like Discord (dedicated server for stateful connections)
  3. Accept the delay for now, optimize later

**Shaw's guidance:**

* For any WebSocket service, use the Gateway pattern similar to Discord
* "It's not like everything has to be serverless" - just avoid idle pods doing nothing
* Gateway service can scale, handles WebSocket → turns into HTTP to agent

## Assignee

Ben

**From today's meeting (Shaw):**

* "Webhooks are basically everything except for the stateful sessions like WebSockets"
* Telegram is webhooks, WhatsApp is webhooks, Twilio/Blue.io are webhooks
* Discord can be webhooks too (but limited to slash commands)
* We might already have webhook registry code - Shaw wrote it, may need to check if it's in cloud
* Should be generic enough that any plugin can use it
* After Discord Gateway, Hanzla should look at this

**P0 - After Discord Gateway**1b:T46f,## Summary

Add visibility into repositories in the org that aren't being scored by the analytics pipeline.

## Two Parts

### 1. MCP Tool (simple, do now)
Add `list_untracked_repos` tool - just expose what's in `untracked_repositories` table.

### 2. Pipeline Enhancement (future)
Run light analytics on ALL org repos periodically (monthly?):
- Last commit/activity date
- Stars, forks
- Open issues/PRs count
- Recent contributor count

This enables data-driven decisions:
```
"Show untracked repos with activity in the last 30 days"
→ Found 2: experiment (15 commits), new-plugin (8 PRs)  
→ Recommend: add to full tracking

"Show stale untracked repos"
→ 12 repos with no activity in 6+ months
→ No action needed
```

## Use Cases

- Audit coverage: "What % of org repos are we tracking?"
- Find active repos: "Any untracked repos with recent PRs?"
- Cross-reference: Issues/PRs in tracked repos might reference untracked repos
- Data-driven tracking decisions without external API calls

**P1 - Post-MVP, starts Tuesday**22:Tc87,## Summary
- **Fix credential injection**: fuzzy Api suffix matching in credentialResolver + correct LLM prompt examples (`gmailOAuth2Api` → `gmailOAuth2`)
- **Fix intent classification**: language-agnostic structural rules instead of keyword-based matching — short confirmations like "creer le" no longer misclassify as "new"
- **Fix DELETE/ACTIVATE/DEACTIVATE**: fetch workflows directly from service instead of relying on `state.data.workflows` (provider data not reliably merged into state by ElizaOS)
- **Fix settings access**: use `runtime.character.settings.workflows` instead of `runtime.getSetting()` which only returns primitives
- **Auto-activate workflows** after deployment via `POST /workflows/{id}/activate`
- **Remove LIST_N8N_WORKFLOWS action**: `activeWorkflowsProvider` already injects workflow list into LLM context (bootstrap plugin pattern)
- **Add safety net** in CREATE "new" path: restore draft if generation fails
- **Rename settings key** from `n8n` to `workflows`
- **Update node catalog** via new `crawl-nodes` script
- **Cloud bridge credential_data flow**: plugin creates n8n credentials from OAuth tokens/API keys provided by cloud bridge, caches mapping in credential store
- **Integration availability check** (spec 09): three-tier pre-generation check — immediate block when no service nodes remain, LLM feasibility assessment when partial coverage exists, proceed when all integrations are available. Prevents generating workflows the platform can't run
- **Conversation context preservation**: `buildConversationContext` passes last 5 messages to generation, preventing context loss after errors or multi-turn interactions
- **Constrain generation to catalog**: LLM prompt now enforces strict usage of provided node types only

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->23:T949,## Problem

Users have no visibility into repositories in their organizations that aren't being tracked for contributor analytics. With 35 tracked repos but 286 total repos across `elizaos` and `elizaos-plugins` orgs, there's a lot of "dark matter" - repos that exist but aren't on the radar.

## Solution

Adds **untracked repository discovery** feature that:

- Discovers all repos in configured organizations
- Shows basic stats: stars, forks, recent PR/issue activity
- Calculates activity scores to surface active repos
- Helps identify which repos might be worth adding to full tracking
- Provides 360-degree view of org activity

**UI:** New collapsible section on `/repos` page showing untracked repos with:
- Repository metadata (description, language, stars)
- Recent activity counts (PRs, issues in last 30 days)
- Activity score ranking
- Links to GitHub

**Pipeline:** `bun run pipeline ingest-untracked`
- Delta detection: only updates repos with changes
- Efficient batch operations
- Configurable org list and exclusion patterns

## Configuration

```json
{
  "PIPELINE_UNTRACKED_REPOS": {
    "enabled": true,
    "organizations": ["elizaos", "elizaos-plugins"],
    "excludeArchived": true,
    "excludePatterns": ["deprecated-*", "test-*"]
  }
}
```

## Implementation

Single-file pipeline (`discoverUntrackedRepos.ts`) using:
- Log-scale activity scoring (prevents big repos from dominating)
- Delta detection via `lastUpdatedAt` (skips unchanged repos)
- Batch database operations
- Existing codebase patterns and utilities

Closes #230

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->24:T78a,# Docker Infrastructure for elizaOS - foundation stage

Adds Docker support with CLI integration and organized target structure for both starter projects and monorepo development.

## New Commands

```bash
# Development with hot reload
elizaos dev --docker

# Production deployment  
elizaos start --docker
```

## How It Works

### Starter Project Context
When using `elizaos create my-project`, the generated project includes Docker configs:

```bash
elizaos create my-project
cd my-project
elizaos dev --docker    # Starts containerized dev environment
elizaos start --docker  # Starts production-ready container
```

**Benefits:**
- **Consistent environments** across team members
- **No local dependency conflicts** (Node versions, system packages)
- **One-command setup** for new developers
- **Production-like testing** locally

### Monorepo Context
For ElizaOS core development, use organized Docker targets:

```bash
# Development
cd docker/targets/dev && docker-compose up

# Production
cd docker/targets/prod && docker-compose up

# Documentation
cd docker/targets/docs && docker-compose up
```
## Structure

```
docker/targets/
├── dev/     # Development: hot reload, debug ports, volume mounting
├── prod/    # Production: optimized builds, health checks, PostgreSQL
└── docs/    # Documentation: fast nginx serving
```

## Testing

```bash
cd docker/tests && bun test

# Test CLI integration
elizaos create test-project
cd test-project
elizaos dev --docker
```

## Compatibility

- No breaking changes
- TEE functionality preserved (`tee-docker-compose.yaml`)
- Project starter templates include Docker configs 

## Next

- reduce prod image size futher, use `docker-slim`
- take prod image and apply to docker registry
- build out `elizaos deploy`, or similar

> [!NOTE]
> **High Risk**
> High risk because this is a breaking change that rewires MCP tool invocation and mutates runtime action registration, plus adds new caching/lazy-connection paths that can affect startup and tool availability.
> 
> **Overview**
> **BREAKING:** Removes the monolithic `CALL_MCP_TOOL` action and instead dynamically registers one native ElizaOS action per MCP tool (with generated names/similes, schema-derived `parameters`, and runtime param validation) so tool execution no longer depends on an LLM tool-selection/argument step.
> 
> Adds an optional Upstash REST schema cache (`McpSchemaCache`) and *lazy connection* behavior: tool schemas can be loaded from cache and actions registered without connecting to MCP until first use; `McpService` is refactored accordingly (connect/disconnect, action (un)registration, updated ping/reconnect handling).
> 
> Includes supporting utilities/refactors (schema conversion, action naming, simplified tool-compatibility exports/detection, improved JSON extraction and attachment IDs, streamlined error/processing code), removes legacy selection/templates/helpers, bumps to `1.8.0`, and adds comprehensive Bun tests.
>

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR transforms the MCP plugin architecture from a multi-step tool invocation pattern to native dynamic action registration, achieving significant performance improvements and code quality gains.

## Key Changes

**Breaking Architecture Change:**
- Removed single `CALL_MCP_TOOL` action that required 3 LLM calls (tool selection → argument generation → execution)
- Replaced with dynamically registered native ElizaOS actions for each MCP tool (1 LLM call per invocation)
- Each MCP tool now appears as a first-class action with proper parameters, validation, and metadata

**Performance Optimizations:**
- Added optional Redis schema caching via Upstash REST API (set `MCP_CACHE_REDIS_URL` to enable)
- Caches tool schemas with config hash validation to avoid MCP connection on cold starts
- Lazy connection support: registers actions from cache before establishing actual MCP connection

**Bug Fixes:**
- Fixed `parseJSON` to handle arrays and use bracket depth counting instead of regex
- Fixed `hashConfig` to recursively sort object keys for consistent hashing
- Fixed validation to properly fail on missing required parameters (was only logging warnings)
- Fixed `processToolResult` to generate unique attachment IDs using incrementing index

**Code Quality Improvements:**
- Removed ~1,000 lines of dead/over-engineered code
- Deleted unused `McpError` class with 5 factory methods
- Consolidated 4 separate constraint interfaces into inline types  
- Extracted base class to break circular dependencies in tool-compatibility
- Replaced `eval('require')` with static imports for ESM compatibility
- Added error logging to previously silent catch blocks

**Testing:**
- Added comprehensive test suite: 204 tests with 388 assertions
- Tests cover action creation, schema conversion, caching, JSON parsing, and integration scenarios
- 63 tests pass in isolated environment (others require `@elizaos/core` dependency)

<h3>Confidence Score: 5/5</h3>

- This PR is safe to merge with minimal risk - well-tested breaking change with clear migration path
- The breaking change is intentional and well-documented. The refactor removes significant amounts of dead code, adds comprehensive test coverage (204 tests), includes important bug fixes, and demonstrates successful deployment on eliza-cloud-v2. The architectural change from 3 LLM calls to 1 is a significant improvement that justifies the breaking change marker.
- No files require special attention - the refactor is well-structured with comprehensive test coverage

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| src/actions/dynamic-tool-actions.ts | New factory for converting MCP tools to native ElizaOS actions - clean implementation with proper validation and error handling |
| src/utils/schema-converter.ts | Converts JSON Schema to ActionParameter format with comprehensive validation and type mapping |
| src/cache/schema-cache.ts | Optional Redis caching for tool schemas with graceful fallback - includes config hash validation |
| src/service.ts | Major refactor to register MCP tools as native actions, adds cache support, removes ~500 lines of dead code |
| src/utils/json.ts | Fixed parseJSON to handle arrays and use bracket depth counting instead of regex |
| src/utils/processing.ts | Fixed processToolResult to generate unique attachment IDs using incrementing index |
| src/tool-compatibility/base.ts | Extracted base class to break circular dependencies, uses static imports for ESM compatibility |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant Runtime
    participant McpService
    participant Cache as SchemaCache
    participant MCP as MCP Server
    participant Action as Dynamic Action

    Note over Runtime,McpService: Initialization (Cold Start)
    Runtime->>McpService: start()
    McpService->>Cache: getSchemas(agentId, serverName, hash)
    alt Cache Hit
        Cache-->>McpService: Return cached tools
        McpService->>McpService: registerToolsAsActions()
        McpService->>McpService: Store lazy connection config
        Note over McpService: Server not connected yet
    else Cache Miss
        McpService->>MCP: connect()
        MCP-->>McpService: Return tools
        McpService->>McpService: registerToolsAsActions()
        McpService->>Cache: setSchemas(agentId, serverName, hash, tools)
    end
    McpService-->>Runtime: Ready (actions registered)

    Note over User,Action: Tool Invocation (1 LLM call)
    User->>Runtime: "Use search tool with query=hello"
    Runtime->>Runtime: LLM selects GOOGLE_SEARCH action
    Runtime->>Action: handler(runtime, message, state)
    Action->>Action: extractParams(message, state)
    Action->>Action: validateParamsAgainstSchema(params)
    alt Lazy Connection
        Action->>McpService: callTool() triggers connection
        McpService->>MCP: connect()
        MCP-->>McpService: Connected
    end
    Action->>McpService: callTool(serverName, toolName, params)
    McpService->>MCP: Execute tool
    MCP-->>McpService: Tool result
    McpService-->>Action: Result with content
    Action->>Action: processToolResult()
    Action-->>Runtime: ActionResult
    Runtime-->>User: Response with tool output
```

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR implements a fully functional broker authentication mode for Twitter, replacing the previous stub implementation. The broker mode allows agents to obtain short-lived OAuth2 access tokens from a centralized broker service by calling `GET {TWITTER_BROKER_URL}/v1/twitter/access-token` with `Authorization: Bearer {TWITTER_BROKER_API_KEY}`.

**Key Changes:**
- Implemented complete `BrokerAuthProvider` with comprehensive error handling, URL validation, and response validation
- Added `TWITTER_BROKER_API_KEY` to environment schema and configuration validation
- Updated documentation to prioritize enterprise/broker setup over developer account setup
- Added 100% coverage threshold and comprehensive offline test suite (unit + E2E) using mock broker and Twitter API servers
- Included mock broker script for local development and testing
- Added `.cursorrules` to document project standards for testing, error messaging, and broker implementation

**Testing:**
All tests run offline with mocked services. The PR includes unit tests for all error paths and E2E tests with real HTTP servers to validate the full broker authentication flow.

**Note:**
The `expires_at` field from the broker response is validated but not currently used for token caching or refresh logic. Consider storing this for future optimization.

- This PR is safe to merge with minimal risk
- Score reflects thorough implementation with comprehensive test coverage (100% threshold), clear error handling, backward compatibility, excellent documentation updates, and adherence to offline testing principles. The broker implementation is well-isolated and follows the existing auth provider pattern.

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Agent as Eliza Agent
    participant Plugin as Twitter Plugin
    participant Broker as Token Broker
    participant Twitter as Twitter API v2

    Note over Agent,Twitter: Broker Auth Mode Flow

    Agent->>Plugin: Initialize with broker mode
    Plugin->>Plugin: Validate broker URL and API key
    
    Agent->>Plugin: Post tweet or API action
    Plugin->>Plugin: getAccessToken() via BrokerAuthProvider
    
    Plugin->>Broker: GET /v1/twitter/access-token<br/>Authorization: Bearer [agent key]
    
    alt Valid API Key
        Broker->>Broker: Validate agent credentials
        Broker->>Broker: Generate/fetch OAuth2 token
        Broker-->>Plugin: 200 OK<br/>{access_token, expires_at}
        Plugin->>Plugin: Validate response fields
        Plugin->>Twitter: Twitter API call<br/>Authorization: Bearer [token]
        Twitter-->>Plugin: Success
        Plugin-->>Agent: Success
    else Invalid API Key
        Broker-->>Plugin: 401/403 Unauthorized
        Plugin->>Plugin: Throw error with hint
        Plugin-->>Agent: Error: Check broker API key
    else Network/Server Error
        Broker-->>Plugin: 500/timeout
        Plugin->>Plugin: Throw error with details
        Plugin-->>Agent: Error: Failed to reach broker
    end
```

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->2a:T7d3,## Summary
- **N8nCredentialStore service**: DB-backed (Drizzle pgSchema) credential mapping store, replacing the in-memory `credentialIdCache` Map. Designed so cloud can override via `runtime.getService('n8n_credential_store')`.
- **Remove listCredentials**: n8n public API returns 405 on `GET /credentials` — action, service method, API method, and all test references removed.
- **Clean OAuthService type**: Removed `getN8nCredId`/`setN8nCredId` that coupled to cloud implementation.
- **Fix code quality**: Dead code paths in `api.ts` and `workflow.ts`, dead test imports, weak assertions strengthened.
- **Bump to 1.0.3**

## New files
- `src/db/schema.ts` — `n8n_workflow.credential_mappings` table (Drizzle pgSchema)
- `src/db/index.ts` — schema re-exports
- `src/services/n8n-credential-store.ts` — default DB-backed implementation

## Cloud override pattern
The plugin registers `N8nCredentialStore` with `serviceType = 'n8n_credential_store'`. On the cloud, a separate plugin registers its own implementation under the same type. `runtime.getService()` returns the first registered, so the cloud implementation wins.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->2c:T1f1e,# Relates to
Fixes runtime errors in the python & rust a2a server example.

# Risks
Low. Changes are isolated to the python package and the a2a example server. The fixes ensure correct handling of protobuf messages which strictly enforces typing and structure, reducing the risk of runtime type errors.

# Background

## What does this PR do?
This PR fixes type compatibility issues in the python package and resolves critical runtime exceptions (`AttributeError`, `TypeError`, `ValueError`) preventing the python a2a server from running. It updates the `elizaos` core package and the `inmemorydb` plugin to correctly interface with generated Protobuf classes, which do not support dynamic dictionary-style access. It also cleans up the a2a example's dependencies and configuration.

It also addresses critical runtime failures in the rust a2a example server: including database initialization, missing core actions, and model configuration bugs.

## What kind of change is this?
- Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)
- Improvements (misc. changes to existing features)

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?
The python a2a server example was failing to start and respond to requests because the code treated strict Protobuf message objects as flexible python dictionaries. This led to failures when assigning values to `State` objects or serializing `Memory` objects. These fixes make the Python implementation functional and compatible with the generated Protobuf types.

Also the rust a2a server example crashes on startup or hangs during execution due to broken dependencies (custom DB adapter, missing `bootstrap` actions) and invalid default configuration (plugin defaulting to `gpt-5`). This PR makes the example functional again.

# Documentation changes needed?
- My changes require a change to the project documentation.
- If documentation change is needed: I have updated the documentation accordingly. (Updated `examples/a2a/python/README.md`)

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?
- `packages/python/elizaos/runtime.py`: Verify the logic change for updating `state.values` and initializing `state.data.providers`.
- `examples/a2a/python/server.py`: Check the field name correction and simplified imports.
- `plugins/plugin-inmemorydb/python/elizaos_plugin_inmemorydb/adapter.py`: Review the `MessageToDict` fix for serializing Memory objects.
- `packages/python/elizaos/types/database.py`: New type definitions.
- - `examples/a2a/rust/src/main.rs`: Core logic changes for the Rust server.

## Detailed testing steps

### python

1. Use the updated installation instructions in `examples/a2a/python/README.md` to set up the environment.
2. Run the server: `python examples/a2a/python/server.py`
3. Verify startup works without import errors.
4. Send a test chat message via curl:
   ```bash
   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/chat \
        -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
        -d '{"message": "Hello"}'
   ```
5. Confirm a JSON response is received (e.g., from `eliza-classic` or OpenAI if configured).

### rust

1. Set `OPENAI_API_KEY` in .env or terminal and run.
2. Run `cargo run --release` in `examples/a2a/rust`.
3. Send a test chat message via curl:
   ```bash
   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/chat \
        -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
        -d '{"message": "Hello"}'
   ```
4. Confirm a JSON response is received (e.g., from `eliza-classic` or OpenAI if configured).

N/A

> **Medium Risk**
> Touches MCP connection/transport initialization, timeouts, and reconnection behavior, so failures could break tool availability or change runtime error handling. Changes are contained to the MCP plugin but impact all tool calls over HTTP/SSE.
> 
> **Overview**
> Adds `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` support for HTTP MCP servers (defaulting non-`sse` HTTP configs to streamable HTTP) and allows optional per-server `headers` to be sent on transport creation for authenticated endpoints.
> 
> Tightens and simplifies `McpService` lifecycle/robustness: explicit 60s connect timeout, clearer error formatting via `errMsg`, ping monitoring only for `stdio` transports, and `restartConnection` now throws if the server isn’t connected. Provider fetching now waits for MCP initialization and returns a consistent empty provider when unavailable; provider/memory text formatting and `CALL_MCP_TOOL` action logging were also refined, and the package version bumps to `1.7.1`.

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR adds `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` support for non-SSE HTTP connections, enables custom header injection for authentication, and performs significant code cleanup.

**Major changes:**
- Added `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` as an alternative to SSE (avoids Redis dependency)
- Added `headers` field to `HttpMcpServerConfig` for authentication token injection
- Introduced `errMsg` helper to standardize error message extraction (~10+ patterns deduplicated)
- Removed ~250 lines of verbose logging and redundant code
- Fixed `restartConnection` to throw when connection is missing (previously silent failure)
- Added 60s timeout to connection initialization with `Promise.race`
- Simplified provider data building and error handling

**Issues found:**
- `src/types.ts` is missing the `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` import and type union update - this will cause TypeScript compilation errors
- Some catch blocks silently swallow errors without logging (minor debugging impact)
- `errMsg` helper is defined before imports (style inconsistency)

The changes align well with the stated goals of reducing Redis requirements and enabling authenticated MCP endpoints. The code quality improvements make the codebase more maintainable.

<h3>Confidence Score: 2/5</h3>

- TypeScript compilation will fail due to missing type imports and union update in types.ts
- The PR introduces critical type errors that will prevent compilation - StreamableHTTPClientTransport is used but not imported in types.ts, and the McpConnection.transport union type is incomplete. While the logic changes are sound and well-tested according to the test plan, the type system errors must be fixed before merge.
- Fix `src/types.ts` to add StreamableHTTPClientTransport import and update the transport union type on line 74

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| src/types.ts | Added headers support to HttpMcpServerConfig, but missing StreamableHTTPClientTransport import and type union update |
| src/service.ts | Added StreamableHTTPClientTransport support, errMsg helper, code cleanup. Error helper defined before imports, some silent error catches could use logging |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Client as ElizaOS Client
    participant Service as McpService
    participant Transport as StreamableHTTPClientTransport
    participant Server as MCP Server

    Client->>Service: initializeMcpServers()
    Service->>Service: getMcpSettings()
    Service->>Service: updateServerConnections(configs)
    
    loop For each server config
        Service->>Service: initializeConnection(name, config)
        Service->>Service: buildHttpClientTransport(name, config)
        
        alt config.type === 'sse'
            Service->>Transport: new SSEClientTransport(url, opts)
            Note over Service: Warns: SSE requires Redis
        else config.type === 'http' | 'streamable-http'
            Service->>Transport: new StreamableHTTPClientTransport(url, opts)
            Note over Transport: Supports custom headers for auth
        end
        
        Service->>Transport: client.connect(transport)
        Transport->>Server: HTTP Connection (with headers)
        Server-->>Transport: Connection established
        
        Service->>Server: fetchToolsList()
        Server-->>Service: Tools list
        
        Service->>Server: fetchResourcesList()
        Server-->>Service: Resources list
        
        Service->>Service: startPingMonitoring()
        Note over Service: Only for stdio, HTTP is stateless
    end
    
    Service->>Service: buildMcpProviderData(servers)
    Service-->>Client: Initialization complete

    Client->>Service: callTool(serverName, toolName, args)
    Service->>Transport: client.callTool(request, timeout)
    Transport->>Server: HTTP Request (with auth headers)
    Server-->>Transport: Tool result
    Transport-->>Service: CallToolResult
    Service-->>Client: Result
```

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR upgrades recharts from version 2.15.2 to 3.6.0, a major version bump that brings new features, bug fixes, and deprecations.

## Key Changes in recharts 3.6.0

**New Features:**
- New `BarStack` component for configuring stacked bar settings
- Support for ranged stacked bars
- New `equidistantPreserveEnd` interval option for XAxis/YAxis
- Additional TypeScript exports (`TooltipIndex`, `BarRectangleItem`, `DataKey`, etc.)
- `graphicalItemId` added to tooltip payload

**Bug Fixes:**
- Fixed Scatter tooltip payload duplication (not applicable - no Scatter usage)
- Fixed Label/LabelList crash
- Numbers are now rounded before DOM insertion
- Improved shadow DOM support with refs instead of IDs

**Deprecations:**
- `CartesianAxis` is deprecated (not used in this codebase)

## Codebase Impact Assessment

The codebase uses recharts in 3 files:
1. **src/components/ui/chart.tsx** - Wrapper component using `ResponsiveContainer`, `Tooltip`, `Legend`
2. **src/components/daily-activity.tsx** - Stacked bar chart with `Bar`, `BarChart`, `CartesianGrid`, `XAxis`, `YAxis`
3. **src/app/repos/components/RepositoryCard.tsx** - Line chart with `Line`, `LineChart`, `XAxis`

## Potential Concerns

1. **React 19 Compatibility**: This codebase uses React 19.0.0. Recharts v3.6.0 may not have been explicitly tested with React 19, as React 19 is very recent. This could potentially cause peer dependency warnings or runtime issues.

2. **Major Version Bump**: Moving from 2.x to 3.x typically indicates breaking changes. While the release notes provided don't show obvious breaking changes that affect the current usage patterns, there may be subtle behavioral differences.

3. **XAxis interval prop**: The code uses `interval="preserveStartEnd"` in daily-activity.tsx. The release notes mention a new `equidistantPreserveEnd` option, but it's unclear if `preserveStartEnd` is still valid or if its behavior has changed.

4. **Stacked Bar Radius**: The code uses `radius={[0, 0, 0, 0]}` on individual `Bar` components within a stack. The new `BarStack` component allows configuring radius for the entire stack, which might change how radius is interpreted.

## Recommendations

- The PR checks will run build, lint, and typecheck which should catch most TypeScript incompatibilities
- Consider manual testing of the charts to ensure visual consistency
- Monitor for peer dependency warnings during installation
- If issues arise, the new `BarStack` component could be explored as an alternative to the current stacked bar implementation

### Confidence Score: 3/5

- This PR carries moderate risk due to the major version bump and React 19 compatibility uncertainty
- Score of 3 reflects: (1) Major version upgrade (2.x→3.x) which typically includes breaking changes, (2) Limited but clear documentation of what changed in v3.6.0, (3) Potential React 19 compatibility concerns since React 19 is very recent, (4) Usage is limited to basic chart components (Bar, Line, XAxis, YAxis) which reduces risk surface area, (5) PR checks will validate build and typecheck, (6) No deprecated features are used except possibly uncertainty around interval="preserveStartEnd"
- Pay close attention to src/components/daily-activity.tsx and src/app/repos/components/RepositoryCard.tsx during testing, as these contain the actual chart implementations that may be affected by behavioral changes

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR adds OpenRouter attribution support through two new environment variables (`OPENROUTER_APP_TITLE` and `OPENROUTER_SITE_URL`) that map to the `X-Title` and `HTTP-Referer` headers respectively. These headers enable apps to appear in OpenRouter's public rankings.

**Key Changes:**
- Added `getAttributionHeaders()` helper function in `src/utils/config.ts` that builds header object from the new env vars
- Integrated attribution headers into the OpenRouter provider via `createOpenRouter()` config
- Added comprehensive documentation in README.md with links to OpenRouter rankings and attribution docs
- Added schema definitions in package.json for the new optional parameters

**Critical Issue:**
- `src/models/embedding.ts` still uses the old undocumented env var names (`OPENROUTER_HTTP_REFERER` and `OPENROUTER_X_TITLE`) directly on lines 70-71, bypassing the new helper functions. This creates inconsistency where users must set both old AND new variable names for attribution to work across all model types (AI SDK models vs. embedding model).

<h3>Confidence Score: 2/5</h3>

- This PR has a logical inconsistency that will cause user confusion and incomplete attribution
- The embedding model uses different env var names than the rest of the codebase, creating a split configuration where users need to set 4 env vars total (2 old + 2 new) to get full attribution coverage. This breaks the expected behavior and user experience.
- src/models/embedding.ts requires updates to use the new attribution helper functions for consistency

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| src/models/embedding.ts | Not updated to use new attribution env vars - still uses old `OPENROUTER_HTTP_REFERER`/`OPENROUTER_X_TITLE` causing inconsistency |
| src/utils/config.ts | Added helper functions for attribution headers with proper documentation and conditional logic |
| src/providers/openrouter.ts | Integrated `getAttributionHeaders` into provider configuration, passes headers to AI SDK |

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR upgrades Next.js from 15.4.10 to 16.1.4, crossing a major version boundary. The update brings important bug fixes and performance improvements:

- Fixed linked list bug in LRU cache (`deleteFromLru`)
- Fixed relative same-host redirects in Node middleware
- Updated Turbopack to swc_core v50.2.3 (fixes MDX multibyte character crashes)
- Enabled mimalloc on musl-based Linux distributions for significant performance improvements
- Fixed junction points vs symlinks on Windows

The project uses Next.js with static export (`output: "export"`), React 19, and TypeScript. The major version jump from 15 to 16 could introduce breaking changes, though the PR description focuses on bug fixes. The project should verify that all Next.js features in use (static export, App Router, Server Components) remain compatible with v16.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

- This PR is safe to merge with minimal risk, pending successful CI/CD build verification
- Score of 4 reflects a well-maintained dependency upgrade from a trusted source (Dependabot) with documented bug fixes and improvements. The major version bump from Next.js 15 to 16 introduces some risk, but the project uses stable features (static export, App Router) and has automated PR checks to catch breaking changes. Deducted 1 point because CI build verification is essential before merging a major framework upgrade
- Verify `next.config.js` remains compatible with Next.js 16 static export configuration

<!-- /greptile_comment -->34:T1547,## Summary

`buildHttpClientTransport` currently returns `SSEClientTransport` for all HTTP transport types (`"http"`, `"streamable-http"`, `"sse"`), despite the README and type definitions advertising Streamable HTTP support. This PR implements the actual transport routing and adds custom headers passthrough.

## Key Changes

### Streamable HTTP Transport Support
- Use `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` from `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` for `"http"` and `"streamable-http"` types
- Keep `SSEClientTransport` only for legacy `"sse"` type
- Aligns runtime behavior with the documented config and `HttpMcpServerConfig` type definition

**Before:**

```typescript
// Always returns SSE regardless of config type
return new SSEClientTransport(new URL(config.url));
```

**After:**

```typescript
if (config.type === 'sse') {
  return new SSEClientTransport(new URL(config.url), { requestInit });
}
return new StreamableHTTPClientTransport(new URL(config.url), { requestInit });
```

### Custom Headers Support
- Added `headers?: Record<string, string>` to `HttpMcpServerConfig`
- Passed through to both transports via `requestInit`
- Enables `Authorization`, API key headers, and other custom headers without workarounds

```json
{
  "my-server": {
    "type": "http",
    "url": "https://example.com/mcp",
    "headers": {
      "Authorization": "Bearer my-api-key"
    }
  }
}
```

### Type Updates
- Added `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` to `McpConnection.transport` union type
- Updated local type annotations in `initializeConnection`

## Impact
- **Correctness**: `type: "http"` now actually uses Streamable HTTP instead of SSE
- **No breaking changes**: `type: "sse"` behavior is unchanged
- **Headers**: Allow authenticated MCP server connections using headers

* **New Features**
  * Introduced StreamableHTTPClientTransport as a new option for HTTP and streamable-HTTP MCP server connections
  * HTTP server configurations now support optional custom headers for authentication and advanced use cases

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

Implements actual Streamable HTTP transport routing that was previously advertised but not working - `type: "http"` and `type: "streamable-http"` now correctly use `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` instead of incorrectly using `SSEClientTransport` for all HTTP types.

- Fixes critical bug where all HTTP transport types were using SSE transport regardless of config
- Adds `headers` field to `HttpMcpServerConfig` for passing custom headers (Authorization, API keys, etc.) to both transport types
- Maintains backward compatibility by keeping `type: "sse"` working with `SSEClientTransport`
- Updates type definitions to include `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` in union types

The changes align runtime behavior with documented config options in README and type definitions. No breaking changes - existing SSE configs continue working with deprecation warning.

<h3>Confidence Score: 5/5</h3>

- Safe to merge - fixes documented behavior, maintains backward compatibility, no breaking changes
- Clean implementation that fixes a critical correctness bug where documented transport types weren't working. The code properly routes to correct transport classes, passes headers through standard RequestInit interface, preserves legacy SSE support with deprecation warning, and includes appropriate logging. Type definitions are properly updated.
- No files require special attention

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| src/types.ts | Added `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` to type unions and `headers` field to HTTP config - clean type additions |
| src/service.ts | Implemented proper transport routing: `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` for http/streamable-http, `SSEClientTransport` only for legacy sse |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Client as McpService
    participant Builder as buildHttpClientTransport
    participant SSE as SSEClientTransport
    participant Streamable as StreamableHTTPClientTransport
    participant Server as MCP Server

    Client->>Builder: buildHttpClientTransport(config)
    
    alt config.type === 'sse'
        Builder->>Builder: Log deprecation warning
        Builder->>SSE: new SSEClientTransport(url, {requestInit})
        Note over SSE: Legacy transport<br/>for backward compatibility
        SSE-->>Builder: transport instance
    else config.type === 'http' OR 'streamable-http'
        Builder->>Builder: Log: using Streamable HTTP
        Builder->>Streamable: new StreamableHTTPClientTransport(url, {requestInit})
        Note over Streamable: Modern transport<br/>with custom headers support
        Streamable-->>Builder: transport instance
    end
    
    Builder-->>Client: return transport
    Client->>Server: client.connect(transport)
    
    Note over Client,Server: Headers from config.headers<br/>passed via requestInit
```

<!-- /greptile_comment -->38:T2fb4,Bumps [@commander-js/extra-typings](https://github.com/commander-js/extra-typings) from 12.1.0 to 14.0.0.
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/commander-js/extra-typings/releases"><code>@​commander-js/extra-typings</code>'s releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>v14.0.0</h2>
<h3>Added</h3>
<ul>
<li>support for groups of options and commands in the help using low-level <code>.helpGroup()</code> on <code>Option</code> and <code>Command</code>, and higher-level <code>.optionsGroup()</code> and <code>.commandsGroup()</code> which can be used in chaining way to specify group title for following options/commands (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/commander-js/extra-typings/issues/100">#100</a>)</li>

</ul>
<h3>Changed</h3>
<ul>
<li><em>Breaking:</em> peer dependency on Commander 14.0.x which requires Node.js 20 or higher</li>
<li>internal refactor of <code>Help</code> class adding <code>.formatItemList()</code> and <code>.groupItems()</code> methods (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/commander-js/extra-typings/issues/93">#93</a>)</li>
</ul>

<h2>Changed</h2>
<ul>
<li>peer dependency on Commander 13.1.x</li>
<li>update dependencies</li>
</ul>
<h2>v13.0.0</h2>

<ul>
<li>strongly type <code>.optsWithGlobals()</code> to include inferred globals (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/commander-js/extra-typings/issues/78">#78</a>)</li>
<li>weakly type <code>.getOptionValueSourceWithGlobals()</code> to include inferred globals (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/commander-js/extra-typings/issues/78">#78</a>)</li>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>


This PR updates `@commander-js/extra-typings` from 12.1.0 to 14.0.0, bringing new features like help grouping support and improved TypeScript typing. The update includes breaking changes:

- Requires Commander 14.0.x as peer dependency (currently v12.0.0 is specified)
- Requires Node.js 20+ (project uses Bun which is compatible)
- Requires TypeScript 5.0+ (project has TS 5.7.3 ✓)

The package is used in two CLI tools (`cli/analyze-pipeline.ts` and `cli/data-sync.ts`) via the `Command` import. The code usage is straightforward and doesn't use any deprecated APIs, so the upgrade should be smooth once the peer dependency is resolved.

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>


- This PR requires the `commander` package to be updated to v14.x to satisfy peer dependency requirements before merging
- The dependency update is well-documented and the breaking changes are clearly outlined in the release notes. However, there's a peer dependency mismatch with the `commander` package (requires 14.x, currently using 12.x) that must be resolved. The TypeScript and runtime requirements are already satisfied. Once the `commander` package is updated, this should be safe to merge.
- Check `package.json` to ensure `commander` is updated to v14.x to match the peer dependency requirement

<!-- /greptile_comment -->3a:T37e2,Bumps [date-fns](https://github.com/date-fns/date-fns) from 3.6.0 to 4.1.0.
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/date-fns/date-fns/releases">date-fns's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>v4.1.0</h2>
<p>This release adds time zone support to format functions (that I somehow missed when working on the feature) and fixes a few bugs.</p>
<p>Make sure also upgrade <code>TZDate</code> to v1.0.2 as it <a href="https://github.com/date-fns/tz/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md#v102---2024-09-14">includes a bunch of critical bug fixes</a>.</p>
<h3>Fixed</h3>
<ul>
<li>Fixed internal <code>constructFrom</code> throwing an exception on <code>null</code> arguments. While <code>null</code> isn't allowed, the functions should rather return <code>Invalid Date</code> or <code>NaN</code> in such cases. See <a href="https://redirect.github.com/date-fns/date-fns/issues/3885">#3885</a>.</li>
</ul>
<h3>Added</h3>
<ul>
<li>Added missing time zone support to <code>format</code>, <code>formatISO</code>, <code>formatISO9075</code>, <code>formatRelative</code> and <code>formatRFC3339</code>. See <a href="https://redirect.github.com/date-fns/date-fns/issues/3886">#3886</a>.</li>
</ul>
<h2>v4.0.0</h2>
<p>I have great news! First, ten years after its release, date-fns finally gets first-class time zone support.</p>
<p>Another great news is that there aren't many breaking changes in this release. All of them are type-related and will affect only those explicitly using internal date-fns types. Finally, it has been less than a year since the last major release, which is an improvement over the previous four years between v2 and v3. I plan on keeping the pace and minimizing breaking changes moving forward.</p>
<p><a href="https://blog.date-fns.org/v40-with-time-zone-support/">Read more about the release in the announcement blog post</a>.</p>
<p>- <a href="https://twitter.com/kossnocorp"><code>Sasha @​kossnocorp</code></a></p>
<h3>Added</h3>
<ul>
<li>
<p>Added time zones support via <a href="https://github.com/date-fns/tz"><code>@date-fns/tz</code></a>'s <code>TZDate</code> class and <code>tz</code> helper function. See its <a href="https://github.com/date-fns/tz">README</a> for the details about the API.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>All relevant functions now accept the context <code>in</code> option, which allows to specify the time zone to make the calculations in. If the function also returns a date, it will be in the specified time zone:</p>
<pre lang="ts"><code>import { addDays, startOfDay } from &quot;date-fns&quot;;
import { tz } from &quot;@date-fns/tz&quot;;
<p>startOfDay(addDays(Date.now(), 5, { in: tz(&quot;Asia/Singapore&quot;) }));<br />
//=&gt; &quot;2024-09-16T00:00:00.000+08:00&quot;<br />
</code></pre></p>
<p>In the example, <code>addDays</code> will get the current date and time in Singapore and add 5 days to it. <code>startOfDay</code> will inherit the date type and return the start of the day in Singapore.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<h3>Changed</h3>
<ul>
<li>
<p>The function arguments, as well as <code>Interval</code>'s <code>start</code> and <code>end</code>, now can be of different types, allowing you to mix <code>UTCDate</code>, <code>TZDate</code>, <code>Date</code>, and other extensions, as well as primitives (strings and numbers).</p>
<p>The functions will normalize these values, make calculations, and return the result in the same type, preventing any bugs caused by the discrepancy. If passed, the type will be inferred from the context <code>in</code> option or the first encountered argument object type. The <code>Interval</code>'s <code>start</code> and <code>end</code> will be considered separately, starting from <code>start</code>.</p>
<p>In the given example, the result will be in the <code>TZDate</code> as the first argument is a number, and the <code>start</code> takes precedence over the <code>end</code>.</p>
<pre lang="ts"><code>clamp(Date.now(), {
  start: new TZDate(start, &quot;Asia/Singapore&quot;),
  end: new UTCDate(),
</code></pre>
</li>
</ul>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>
<p>... (truncated)</p>
</details>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>


Dependency upgrade bumping `date-fns` from v3.6.0 to v4.1.0. This is a major version update that introduces first-class time zone support and several improvements.

Key changes in date-fns v4:
- Added time zone support via `TZDate` class and `in` option for context
- Functions can now accept mixed date types (`UTCDate`, `TZDate`, `Date`, primitives)
- Fixed `constructFrom` bug with null arguments
- Breaking changes are primarily type-related affecting internal date-fns types only

The codebase primarily uses:
- `addDays`, `subDays`, `addMonths`, `differenceInDays`, `formatDate` from `date-fns`
- `UTCDate` from `@date-fns/utc` (separate package, unaffected)
- All usage patterns appear compatible with v4 API

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>


- This PR is safe to merge with low risk
- This is an automated dependency upgrade to a major version with mostly backward-compatible changes. The breaking changes are type-related and don't affect the function APIs used in this codebase. The codebase uses basic date manipulation functions (addDays, subDays, formatDate) which have stable APIs. The project already uses `@date-fns/utc` package which is separate and unaffected by this upgrade.
- Check that tests pass to verify date manipulation logic still works correctly with the new version

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| package.json | Bumps date-fns from 3.6.0 to 4.1.0, a major version upgrade with time zone support and bug fixes |

<h2>v14.0.2</h2>

<li>update (dev) dependencies</li>
</ul>
<h2>v14.0.0</h2>
<h3>Added</h3>
<ul>
<li>support for groups of options and commands in the help using low-level <code>.helpGroup()</code> on <code>Option</code> and <code>Command</code>, and higher
-level <code>.optionsGroup()</code> and <code>.commandsGroup()</code> which can be used in chaining way to specify group title for following option
s/commands (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tj/commander.js/issues/2328">#2328</a>)</li>
<li>support for unescaped negative numbers as option-arguments and command-arguments (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tj/commander.js/issues/2339">#2339</a>)</li>
<li>TypeScript: add <code>parseArg</code> property to <code>Argument</code> class (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tj/commander.js/issues/2359">#2359</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h3>Fixed</h3>
<ul>
<li>remove bogus leading space in help when option has default value but not a description (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tj/commander.js/issues/2348">#2348</a>)</li>
<li><code>.configureOutput()</code> now makes copy of settings instead of modifying in-place, fixing side-effects (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tj/commander.js/issues/2350">#2350</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h3>Changed</h3>
<ul>
<li><em>Breaking:</em> Commander 14 requires Node.js v20 or higher</li>
<li>internal refactor of <code>Help</code> class adding <code>.formatItemList()</code> and <code>.groupItems()</code> methods (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tj/commander.js/issues/2328">#2328</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>v13.1.0</h2>
<h3>Added</h3>
<ul>
<li>support a pair of long option flags to allow a memorable shortened flag, like <code>.option('--ws, --workspace')</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tj/commander.js/issues/2312">#2312</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>v13.0.0</h2>
<h3>Added</h3>
<ul>
<li>support multiple calls to <code>.parse()</code> with default settings (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tj/commander.js/issues/2299">#2299</a>)</li>
<li>add <code>.saveStateBeforeParse()</code> and <code>.restoreStateBeforeParse()</code> for use by subclasses (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tj/commander.js/issues/2299">#2299</a>)</li>
<li>style routines like <code>styleTitle()</code> to add color to help using <code>.configureHelp()</code> or Help subclass (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tj/commander.js/issues/2251">#2251</a>)</li>
</ul>

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR updates `commander` from v12.1.0 to v14.0.2, a major version bump that includes breaking changes and new features.

**Key Changes:**
- Commander v14 now requires Node.js v20 or higher (breaking change)
- Adds support for option/command grouping in help output
- Adds support for unescaped negative numbers as arguments
- Multiple bug fixes and code quality improvements

**Compatibility Assessment:**
The codebase uses `@commander-js/extra-typings` (already at v14.0.0) and imports from it in `cli/analyze-pipeline.ts:29` and `cli/data-sync.ts:15`. The project runs on Bun (latest version in CI workflows), which supports Node.js v20+ APIs, so this update should be compatible.

**No Code Changes Needed:**
The usage patterns in the CLI scripts are straightforward and don't use deprecated APIs. The update should work without modifications.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

- This PR is safe to merge with minimal risk. It's a standard dependency update with good compatibility.
- Score reflects that this is a well-maintained dependency with documented breaking changes. The project already uses `@commander-js/extra-typings@14.0.0` which is compatible with `commander@14.0.2`. The runtime (Bun) supports Node.js v20+ APIs required by Commander v14. The straightforward CLI usage patterns don't rely on deprecated APIs. Only minor concern is the major version jump, but CI validation should catch any issues.
- No files require special attention. The dependency update is isolated to `package.json` and doesn't require code changes.

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| package.json | Bumps `commander` from 12.1.0 to 14.0.2. Breaking change: requires Node.js v20+. Project uses Bun runtime which should be compatible. |

- `@dependabot unignore <dependency name> <ignore condition>` will remove the ignore condition of the specified dependency and ignore conditions

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR bumps two dependencies in the minor-and-patch group: `lucide-react` from 0.562.0 to 0.563.0 and `prettier` from 3.8.0 to 3.8.1.

**Changes:**
- `lucide-react` 0.563.0 adds `aria-hidden` by default to all icon components for improved accessibility (already present in lucide-react previously), includes various icon fixes and new icons
- `prettier` 3.8.1 includes TypeScript type declaration improvements for plugin `printers` property

Both updates are backward-compatible with no breaking changes or API modifications.

<h3>Confidence Score: 5/5</h3>

- This PR is safe to merge with minimal risk
- Both dependency updates are minor/patch version bumps with backward-compatible changes only. The lucide-react update adds accessibility improvements and icon fixes, while prettier fixes TypeScript type declarations. No breaking changes, API modifications, or functionality changes that could affect the existing codebase.
- No files require special attention

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>


Automated dependency update from Dependabot upgrading Next.js from 15.4.10 to 16.1.3. This minor version bump includes several bug fixes and performance improvements:

- Fixed linked list bug in LRU cache deleteFromLru operation
- Fixed relative same-host redirects in Node.js middleware
- Updated Turbopack to swc_core v50.2.3 (fixes MDX processing with multibyte characters)
- Enabled mimalloc allocator on musl-based Linux distributions (significant performance improvement for Alpine/Docker environments)
- Fixed junction point creation on Windows instead of symlinks

The codebase is already compatible with Next.js 16 as it uses the async params pattern introduced in Next.js 15 (`params: Promise<>`). The project configuration uses static export mode (`output: "export"`), which is unaffected by these changes.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>


- This PR is safe to merge with low risk
- This is a standard Dependabot upgrade from Next.js 15.4.10 to 16.1.3, a minor version bump that includes bug fixes and performance improvements. The codebase already uses Next.js 15's async params pattern, making it fully compatible with version 16. The changes are limited to a single line in package.json, and the release notes indicate these are backported bug fixes without breaking changes. The project uses static export mode which is stable across these versions.
- No files require special attention

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| package.json | Updated Next.js from 15.4.10 to 16.1.3 with bug fixes for LRU cache, middleware redirects, and performance improvements for Turbopack |

</details>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant D as Dependabot
    participant PM as Package Manager
    participant NX as Next.js 16.1.3
    participant APP as Application Code

    PM->>NX: Install Next.js 16.1.3

This follows Node.js best practices by relying on the package's exports field defined in `@elizaos/cli/package.json`, which maps `.` to `./dist/index.js`.

- Improves consistency with standard Node.js module resolution patterns

<!-- /greptile_comment -->40:Te50,## Plugin Details

**Package:** `agentindex-eliza-plugin`
**Repository:** https://github.com/CIII-glitch/agentindex-eliza-plugin
**NPM:** https://www.npmjs.com/package/agentindex-eliza-plugin

## Description

Agent Index plugin enables Eliza agents to discover x402 endpoints - the payment-enabled APIs in the x402 ecosystem.

### Actions
- `SEARCH_AGENT_INDEX` - Search for x402 endpoints by keyword, category, or price
- `TRENDING_ENDPOINTS` - Get trending x402 endpoints

### Installation
```bash
npm install agentindex-eliza-plugin
```

### Usage
```typescript
import { agentIndexPlugin } from 'agentindex-eliza-plugin';

// Add to your Eliza agent
plugins: [agentIndexPlugin]
```

## Links
- Website: https://theagentindex.app
- API Docs: https://api.theagentindex.app/docs.html
- x402 Protocol: https://x402.org

<!-- /greptile_comment -->41:T18d7,# Overall Project Weekly Summary (Jan 25 - 31, 2026)

This week, ElizaOS focused on stabilizing the developer experience and laying the groundwork for a more powerful, consumer-ready AI application ecosystem. By resolving critical setup blockers and expanding how agents interact with the world—through better security, code execution, and automation—the project is moving from a framework toward a comprehensive service platform.

## Executive Summary
The team successfully restored the project's "front door" by fixing a critical bug that prevented new developers from starting projects. With the foundation stabilized, the focus shifted to strategic expansion, including the development of a new "Eliza App" and the integration of advanced automation tools like N8N to make AI agents more capable in professional environments.

### Key Strategic Initiatives & Outcomes

**Restoring and Improving the Developer Onboarding Experience**
*Goal: To ensure that anyone wanting to build with ElizaOS can get started in minutes without technical errors.*
*   Fixed a major "Project Generation" failure that was blocking new users from creating projects, restoring the primary entry point for the community ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).
*   Updated all official guides to use the correct installation commands, ensuring new developers have a smooth, error-free setup process ([elizaos/docs](https://github.com/elizaos/docs)).

**Expanding Agent Capabilities and Intelligence**
*Goal: To give AI agents the tools they need to perform complex tasks, like writing code or managing business workflows.*
*   Launched a "Code Execution" plugin that allows agents to write and run their own code natively to solve problems ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).
*   Introduced "Broker Authentication" for Twitter, allowing for more secure and professional automated social media management ([elizaos-plugins/plugin-twitter](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-twitter)).
*   Began integrating the N8N workflow engine, which will allow agents to connect to thousands of different business apps and automate complex sequences of tasks ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).

**Strengthening Project Infrastructure and Security**
*Goal: To make the ElizaOS ecosystem more secure, transparent, and easier to manage as it grows to hundreds of sub-projects.*
*   Established a formal security protocol to ensure vulnerabilities can be reported and fixed safely ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).
*   Deployed a new analytics system to track and monitor the health of over 300 different code repositories within the ElizaOS organization ([elizaos/elizaos.github.io](https://github.com/elizaos/elizaos.github.io)).
*   Modernized the core web framework and database tools to improve performance and keep the platform secure against modern threats ([elizaos/elizaos.github.io](https://github.com/elizaos/elizaos.github.io)).

### Cross-Repository Coordination
This week featured a highly synchronized effort to fix a critical system failure that spanned multiple parts of the project:
*   **The "Create" Command Fix**: When the command to start a new project broke, it required a three-pronged solution. The core team fixed the underlying code in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza), the plugin team verified the fix in [elizaos-plugins/plugin-twitter](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-twitter), and the documentation team updated the user guides in [elizaos/docs](https://github.com/elizaos/docs). This coordinated effort ensured that the fix was not just implemented, but also communicated clearly to the community.

---

### elizaos/eliza
*   **Restored Project Creation**: Resolved a critical failure in the `elizaos create` command ([#6388](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6388)) and updated internal pathing to prevent future environment errors ([#6389](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/6389)).
*   **New Agent Tools**: Finalized the **GitHub Plugin** for repository management ([#6406](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6406)) and the **Code Execution Plugin** ([#6408](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6408)).
*   **Strategic Planning**: Initiated research for the "Eliza App" by analyzing market competitors ([#6394](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6394)) and began planning for multi-platform messaging (WhatsApp, Telegram) via a new webhook service ([#6429](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6429)).
*   **Security & Reliability**: Created a formal `SECURITY.md` for vulnerability reporting ([#6428](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/6428)) and resolved a block on AI inference by topping up gateway credits ([#6393](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6393)).
